Given uncertainty, implement a general optimizer with multiple starts and local search. It may find a better sequence if exists. Need ensure it returns best exact-evaluated candidate. Use FFT surrogate for speed.

Let's design program:

```
import numpy as np, time, math, scipy.fft as fft

def lse_conv_score_est(a):
    # a sum 1, returns (score_fft, c, maxc)
    n=len(a); L=1; while L<2*n-1: L<<=1
    fa=fft.rfft(a,L)
    c=fft.irfft(fa*fa,L)[:2*n-1]
    c=np.clip(c,0,None)
    M=c.max()
    S=a.sum()
    score=2*n*M/(S*S) # if not normalized
    return score,c,M
```

But for many evaluations, fastest use normalized and M. For exact, evaluate_sequence direct.

Generate binomial:
```
def binomial_seq(n):
  b=np.empty(n); b[0]=1.0
  for j in range(1,n): b[j]=b[j-1]*(2*j-1)/(4*j)
  b/=b.sum()
  return b
```

Projected gradient implementation:
```
def project_simplex(v):
    # v float array, want v>=0 sum 1
    x = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    css = np.cumsum(x)
    rho = np.nonzero(x - (css - 1)/np.arange(1, len(v)+1) > 0)[0]
    if len(rho)==0: return np.full(len(v),1/len(v))
    rho = rho[-1]
    theta = (css[rho] - 1)/(rho+1)
    y = np.maximum(v - theta, 0)
    y /= y.sum() # correct numerical
    return y
```
Potential issue: if v has large negative, sorting O(n log n) okay. Alternatively binary search to avoid sort? Sorting 7k * maybe 500 = 3.5M log? Actually 7k sort ~0.0005s, 1000=0.5s. Fine.

Gradient step:
- Need `w` length L0=2*n-1. We need convolutions using rfft length L1 for c: Lc >= 2n-1.
- For gradient: cv2 = irfft(rfft(a,L)*rfft(w,L), L)[:n] where L>=L0.
- For T1: a_rev=a[::-1]; Lg >= (n-1)+(2n-1) maybe 3n-2. Use L3 >=3n-2 (or 3n). `conv_av = irfft(rfft(a_rev,L3)*rfft(w_pad,L3), L3)` where w_pad length L3 with w first 2n-1. Slice indices [n-1:2n-1]. This gives T1? Let's test small n=3,a=[a0,a1,a2], w length5 w0..w4. T1_j=∑_{t=0}^{2} a_t w_{j+t}.

For fixed a with normalized sum=1 and M=maxc. Scale so M=1? Let b = a/M? Actually for current a sum=1, c_max=M. Constraints for g: (a*g)_k ≤ M. To maximize sum g. Let h = g? We can scale by M. Solve dual min ∑ y s.t. a_j W_j ≥1, W_j window length n of y. This dual greedy O(n) gives exact optimum V (max sum g) for linearized problem. If V>1, we know possible improvement. But how to get g? We can perhaps derive g from the dual solution via a second greedy primal using complementary slackness, or formulate primal as dual of dual and solve with same greedy? The primal has interval constraints with variable costs a_j, profits 1. Since the constraint matrix is an interval matrix, the LP can be solved by a greedy algorithm too: sort variables by cost a_j? Let's investigate.

Primal:
max ∑ x_j
s.t. for each k=0..2n-2: ∑_{j=max(0,k-n+1)}^{min(k,n-1)} a_j x_j ≤1
x_j≥0.
This is a continuous packing of intervals [j,j+n-1] (reverse index? Each x_j occupies point k with weight a_j). Equivalent to scheduling with resource capacities 1 per time, jobs j require amount a_j at each time in its interval? Profit x_j. Since all jobs have profit 1 per unit, but resource consumption per unit rate a_j. This is like choose rates x_j with fractional interval weights. The dual is min ∑ y_k s.t. ∑_{k∈I_j} a_j y_k ≥1.
For interval matrices, an extreme optimal primal can be found by considering breakpoints where constraints tight. But maybe easier: The dual solution y from greedy has many zeros? The greedy for dual: process j=0..n-1, window [j,j+n-1] lower L_j=1/a_j. It adds at right endpoint j+n-1. So y_k positive at most one for each j? In fact y_{n-1+j} may get increments. Then y has at most n positive positions (one per window), all in last n positions (indices n-1..2n-2). Let's simulate: j=0 add to y_{n-1}; j=1 if y_{n-1} < L_1, add to y_n; etc. But window j includes positions j..j+n-1; greedy adding to right endpoint means positive y at positions n-1, n, ..., 2n-2. So y positive only in second half. Then primal complementary: g_j>0 only if W_j=1/a_j, where W_j=∑_{k=j}^{j+n-1} y_k. With y only second half, W_j is decreasing? Maybe many tight.

import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft
    _HAVE_SCRIPY=True
except Exception:
    _HAVE_SCRIPY=False

def _fft_conv_sq(a):
    n=a.size
    if _HAVE_SCRIPY:
       L=1
       while L < 2*n-1: L <<=1
       fa=rfft(a,L)
       c=irfft(fa*fa,L)[:2*n-1]
       return np.maximum(c,0.0)
    else:
       return np.convolve(a,a) # fallback direct (might be slow but budget)
```

Let's refine projected gradient:

```
def _simplex_project(v):
    x = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    k = np.arange(1, len(v)+1, dtype=np.float64)
    css = np.cumsum(x)
    cond = (x - (css - 1.0)/k) > 0
    rho = np.nonzero(cond)[0]
    if rho.size == 0:
        return np.full(len(v), 1.0/len(v), dtype=np.float64)
    idx = rho[-1]
    theta = (css[idx] - 1.0)/(idx+1)
    y = np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
    s = y.sum()
    if s <=0 or not finite: return best? better fallback binomial normalized.
    return y/s
```
The standard formula uses values sorted descending; if x - (cumsum-1)/i >0. Good.
If v has nan due to numerical, handle.

LSE gradient function:
```
def _lse_grad(a, tau, L, fa=None):
   fa = fa if ... else rfft(a,L)
   c = irfft(fa*fa, L)[:2*n-1]
   c = np.maximum(c,0)
   m = c.max()
   u = (c-m)/tau
   mu = u.max()
   z = np.exp(u-mu)
   zsum = z.sum()
   logZ = mu + math.log(zsum)
   w = np.exp(u - logZ)  # length 2n-1
   fw = rfft(w,L)
   conv_aw = irfft(fa*fw,L)
   t2 = conv_aw[:n]
   a_rev = a[::-1]
   fa_rev = rfft(a_rev,L)
   conv_arw = irfft(fa_rev*fw,L)
   t1 = conv_arw[n-1:2*n-1]
   grad = t1 + t2
   return m + tau*logZ, grad, c
```
Need pad w to L: `w_full = np.zeros(L); w_full[:2*n-1]=w; fw=rfft(w_full,L)`. In rfft, if w length <L, automatically zero padded? scipy rfft(a, n=L) works. For conv_arw, a_rev length n, w padded L. Good.
Numerical: irfft results may have tiny imaginary discarded; negative small in c clip.

def normalize_nonneg(a):
  a = np.asarray(a,float)
  a = np.clip(a,0,1000)
  s=a.sum()
  if s>0: a/=s
  return a

for it in range(max_iters):
   a_trial = best.copy()
   width = rng.integers(n//200, n//10+1)
   start = rng.integers(0, n-width+1)
   mul = np.exp(rng.normal(0, sigma))

Pseudo:
```
def _fft_lse_value(a, tau, L):
    fa=rfft(a,L)
    c=irfft(fa*fa,L)[:2n-1]
    c=np.maximum(c,0)
    m=c.max()
    u=(c-m)/tau
    mu=u.max()
    z=np.exp(u-mu)
    zsum=z.sum()
    val=m+tau*(mu+math.log(zsum))
    return val,m
```
Gradient:
```
def _fft_lse_grad(a,tau,L):
    fa=rfft(a,L)
    c=...; m; u; mu; z; zsum; logZ
    val=m+tau*logZ
    w=np.exp(u-logZ); w_full=np.zeros(L); w_full[:2n-1]=w
    fw=rfft(w_full,L)
    t2=irfft(fa*fw,L)[:n]
    fa_rev=rfft(a[::-1],L)
    t1=irfft(fa_rev*fw,L)[n-1:2n-1]
    grad=t1+t2
    return val,grad,m,c? We only need m.
```

Let's draft code:

```
import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft
except Exception:
    from numpy.fft import rfft, irfft

def _next_pow2(m):
    m=int(m)
    p=1
    while p<m:
        p <<=1
    return p

def _normalize(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64)
    a=np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<=0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(a.size,1.0/max(1,a.size))
    return a/s

def _binomial(n):
    b=np.empty(n,dtype=np.float64)
    if n==0: return b
    b[0]=1.0
    for j in range(1,n):
        b[j] = b[j-1] * (2.0*j - 1.0)/(4.0*j)
    s=b.sum()
    if s>0: b/=s
    return b

def _fft_score(a):
    n=a.size
    s=a.sum()
    if n==0 or s<=0: return (float('inf'),0.0)
    a=a/s
    L=_next_pow2(2*n-1)
    fa=rfft(a,L)
    c=irfft(fa*fa,L)
    c=c[:2*n-1]
    c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
    m=float(c.max())
    return (2.0*n*m, m)
```
But `rfft` from numpy has default n=None; if we pass `a,L` okay. For scipy, `rfft(a,L)`. Good. `irfft` from numpy returns half spectrum length L. Fine.
Potential issue: if `a` is complex? no.

       lst=[float(x) for x in a.tolist()]
       val=evaluate_sequence(lst)
    except Exception:
       val=float('inf')
    return val if np.isfinite(val) else float('inf')
```
But if evaluate_sequence returns np.inf, np.isfinite works.

Projection:
```
def _project_simplex(v):
    v=np.asarray(v,dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
        v=np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    n=v.size
    x=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    i=np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=np.float64)
    css=np.cumsum(x)
    cond = x - (css - 1.0)/i > 0.0
    idx = np.nonzero(cond)[0]
    if idx.size == 0:
        return np.full(n,1.0/n)
    k=idx[-1]
    theta=(css[k]-1.0)/(k+1)
    y=np.maximum(v-theta,0.0)
    sy=y.sum()
    if sy<=0 or not np.isfinite(sy): return np.full(n,1.0/n)
    y/=sy
    return y
```

LSE functions inside propose to capture n,L:
```
def _lse_val(a,tau,L):
   fa=rfft(a,L)
   c=irfft(fa*fa,L)[:2n-1]
   c=np.maximum(c,0.0)
   m=c.max()
   u=(c-m)/tau
   mu=u.max()
   z=np.exp(u-mu)
   zs=z.sum()
   if zs<=0: return float(m), float(m)
   val=m+tau*(mu+math.log(zs))
   return val,m
```
For gradient, need L and n. `w_full=np.zeros(L); w_full[:2*n-1]=w`.

But `math.log(zs)` if zs is np.float64, okay.

In gradient, if `tau` small, `u-logZ` maybe negative large; exp underflow okay. Set `w = np.exp(u - logZ) where finite`.

```
def _lse_grad(a,tau,L):
  fa=rfft(a,L)
  c=irfft(fa*fa,L)[:2n-1]; c=np.maximum(c,0)
  m=c.max(); u=(c-m)/tau; mu=u.max(); z=np.exp(u-mu); zs=z.sum(); logZ=mu+math.log(zs); val=m+tau*logZ
  w=np.exp(u-logZ);
  w_full=np.zeros(L); w_full[:2n-1]=w
  fw=rfft(w_full,L)
  t2=irfft(fa*fw,L)[:n]
  a_rev=a[::-1]; fa_rev=rfft(a_rev,L)
  t1=irfft(fa_rev*fw,L)[n-1:2*n-1]
  grad=t1+t2
  # numerical cleanup
  grad=np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
  return val, grad, m
```
Need ensure `l` length enough for conv of a_rev and w: a_rev length n, w_full length L but actual w length 2n-1. Convolution output length in FFT circular of length L. We need L >= (n-1)+(2n-2)+1 =3n-2. We set L_grad nextpow2(3*n-1). Good. The slice `n-1:2*n-1` length n. For n=1, slice 0:1 okay, L>=? n=1 L_grad nextpow2(2)=2. c len1. t1 slice [0:1]. OK.

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    start=time.monotonic()
    budget=float(budget_s)
    headroom=max(10.0, 0.1*budget)
    deadline=start + max(1.0, budget - headroom)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    # parent
    try:
       parent=np.asarray(height_sequence_1,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
       parent=np.ones(2048,dtype=np.float64)
    parent=np.where(np.isfinite(parent), parent, 0.0)
    parent=np.clip(parent,0.0,1000.0)
    if parent.size<1:
       parent=np.ones(2048)
    # choose n
    max_n=8000
    if parent.size<8: n=max(2,parent.size)
    elif parent.size <= max_n: n=parent.size
    else: n=6962 if 6962<=max_n else max_n? If parent bigger, perhaps choose min(parent.size,6962). Use 6962 to match known.
    n=int(n)
    # fallback best
    best=None; best_score=float('inf')
    def consider(a, do_exact=True):
       nonlocal best,best_score
       a=_normalize(a)
       fft_score,_=_fft_score(a)
       if not np.isfinite(fft_score): return
       # quick prune: if fft_score > best_score + 1e-10 maybe no exact (unless exact not evaluated?)
       if best_score == float('inf') or fft_score < best_score - 1e-9 or do_exact:
           if do_exact:
              score=_exact(a)
              if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score:
                 best_score=score; best=a.copy()
       # If not do exact but fft improves, maybe store best_fft? We'll exact later. Simpler always exact for family few.
```

Local search:
```
if time.monotonic() < deadline and best is not None:
   a=best.copy()
   # ensure sum 1
   a=_normalize(a)
   n=a.size
   L= _next_pow2(3*n-1)
   tau0 = 1e-6 * max(1e-6, 2*n*???) # Use m from fft
   score0,m0=_fft_score(a)
   tau=max(1e-12, 1e-3*m0) # m0 ~1e-4 =>1e-7
   alpha=1.0
   fails=0
   acc=0
   # maybe initial attempt exact best score known.
   max_iter = 20000
   for it in range(max_iter):
      if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
      try:
         val, grad, m = _lse_grad(a, tau, L)
      except Exception:
         break
      if not np.isfinite(val) or not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
         break
      accepted=False
      # If m is not improving, maybe try a random perturbation instead of gradient?
      for step_trial in range(4):
         if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
         trial = a - alpha*grad
         trial = _project_simplex(trial)
         try:
            tval, tm = _lse_val(trial, tau, L)
         except Exception:
            break
         if np.isfinite(tval) and (tm < m - 1e-15 or tval < val - 1e-12):
            a=trial; acc+=1; fails=0; accepted=True; break
         alpha *= 0.6
      if not accepted:
         # try a profitable random perturbation of a?
         if fails > 20:
            # restart from best with small noise
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 20: break
            a = _normalize(best * np.exp(rng.normal(0, 0.05, n)))
            tau = max(1e-12, 1e-3*m)
            alpha=1.0; fails=0; continue
         fails +=1
      else:
         alpha = min(10.0, alpha*1.2)
         if acc % 25 == 0:
             consider(a)
      # anneal tau
      if (it+1) % 50 ==0:
         tau *= 0.9
         if tau < 1e-11: tau=1e-11
   consider(a, force=True)
```

```python
import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    from scipy.fft import rfft, irfft
except Exception:
    from numpy.fft import rfft, irfft


TARGET_SCORE = 1.50274349
DEFAULT_N = 6962
MAX_N = 8192


def _next_pow2(m):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    p = 1
    while p < m:
        p <<= 1
    return p


def _normalize(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full(a.size, 1.0 / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return (a / s).astype(np.float64, copy=False)


def _binomial_length(n):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    b = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b[0] = 1.0
    for j in range(1, n):
        b[j] = b[j - 1] * (2.0 * j - 1.0) / (4.0 * j)
    return _normalize(b)


def _project_simplex(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.where(np.isfinite(v), v, 0.0)
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)

    x = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    i = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    css = np.cumsum(x)
    idx = np.nonzero(x - (css - 1.0) / i > 0.0)[0]
    if idx.size == 0:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)

    k = int(idx[-1])
    theta = (css[k] - 1.0) / float(k + 1)
    y = np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
    s = float(y.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    return y / s


def _fft_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf"), 0.0
    a = _normalize(a)
    L = _next_pow2(2 * n - 1)
    fa = rfft(a, L)
    c = irfft(fa * fa, L)[: 2 * n - 1]
    c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    m = float(c.max())
    return float(2.0 * n * m), m


def _exact_score(a):
    try:
        lst = [float(x) for x in np.asarray(a).ravel()]
        val = evaluate_sequence(lst)
        if np.isfinite(val):
            return float(val)
    except Exception:
        pass
    return float("inf")


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    budget = float(budget_s)
    deadline = start + max(1.0, budget - 15.0)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) if seed is not None else 42)

    best = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    try:
        parent = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        parent = np.ones(DEFAULT_N, dtype=np.float64)

    parent = np.where(np.isfinite(parent), parent, 0.0)
    parent = np.clip(parent, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if parent.size == 0:
        parent = np.ones(DEFAULT_N, dtype=np.float64)

    if 32 <= parent.size <= MAX_N:
        n = int(parent.size)
    else:
        n = DEFAULT_N if DEFAULT_N <= MAX_N else MAX_N

    def consider(a, force=False):
        nonlocal best, best_score
        a = _normalize(a)
        if a.size == 0:
            return
        fft_val, _ = _fft_score(a)
        if not np.isfinite(fft_val):
            return
        need = force or (not np.isfinite(best_score) or fft_val < best_score - 1e-10)
        if need:
            sc = _exact_score(a)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                best_score = float(sc)
                best = a.copy()

    # Parent as a legitimate initial candidate.
    if parent.size <= MAX_N:
        consider(parent, force=True)

    # Strong asymptotically near-optimal initial construction.
    binom = _binomial_length(n)
    consider(binom, force=True)

    # Useful compactly generated variants; exact evaluation only if competitive.
    js = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for delta in (0.25, 0.50, 0.75, 1.00):
        for alpha in (0.45, 0.50, 0.55):
            a = 1.0 / np.power(js + float(delta), alpha)
            consider(a, force=False)

    b = _binomial_length(n)
    for lam in (-6.0 / n, -3.0 / n, 3.0 / n, 6.0 / n):
        a = b * np.exp(lam * js)
        consider(a, force=False)

    if np.isfinite(best_score) and best_score <= TARGET_SCORE + 1e-10:
        final = _normalize(best)
        return [float(x) for x in final]

    # Smooth projected local search around the best exact candidate.
    if best is None:
        best = b
        best_score = _exact_score(best)

    try:
        a = _normalize(best)
        cur_n = int(a.size)
        L = _next_pow2(3 * cur_n - 1)
        _, m0 = _fft_score(a)
        tau = max(1e-12, 1e-3 * max(1e-30, m0))
        alpha = 0.7
        fails = 0
        acc = 0
        it = 0

        def lse_value(x, temperature, Llen):
            fa = rfft(x, Llen)
            c = irfft(fa * fa, Llen)[: 2 * cur_n - 1]
            c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
            m = float(c.max())
            if temperature <= 0.0:
                return m, m
            u = (c - m) / temperature
            mu = float(u.max())
            z = np.exp(u - mu)
            zs = float(z.sum())
            if zs <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(zs):
                return m, m
            val = m + temperature * (mu + math.log(zs))
            return float(val), m

        def lse_grad(x, temperature, Llen):
            nonlocal cur_n
            fa = rfft(x, Llen)
            c = irfft(fa * fa, Llen)[: 2 * cur_n - 1]
            c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
            m = float(c.max())
            u = (c - m) / temperature
            mu = float(u.max())
            z = np.exp(u - mu)
            zs = float(z.sum())
            if zs <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(zs):
                return m, np.zeros_like(x), m
            logZ = mu + math.log(zs)
            val = m + temperature * logZ
            w = np.exp(u - logZ)
            wfull = np.zeros(Llen, dtype=np.float64)
            wfull[: 2 * cur_n - 1] = w
            fw = rfft(wfull, Llen)

            t2 = irfft(fa * fw, Llen)[:cur_n]
            arev = x[::-1]
            farev = rfft(arev, Llen)
            t1 = irfft(farev * fw, Llen)[cur_n - 1 : 2 * cur_n - 1]
            grad = t1 + t2
            grad = np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
            return float(val), grad, m

        while time.monotonic() < deadline:
            it += 1
            if (it & 7) == 0 and time.monotonic() > deadline:
                break

            val, grad, m = lse_value_and_grad = None, None, None
            try:
                val, grad, m = lse_grad(a, tau, L)
            except Exception:
                break
            if not np.isfinite(val) or m is None or (not np.all(np.isfinite(grad))):
                break

            accepted = False
            for _ attempt in range(6):
                if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                    break
                trial = a - alpha * grad
                trial = _project_simplex(trial)
                try:
                    tval, tm = lse_value(trial, tau, L)
                except Exception:
                    break
                if np.isfinite(tval) and (tval < val - 1e-12 or tm < m - 1e-15):
                    a = trial
                    val, m = tval, tm
                    accepted = True
                    acc += 1
                    fails = 0
                    break
                alpha *= 0.62

            if not accepted:
                fails += 1
                if alpha < 1e-8:
                    alpha = 0.7
                    fails = min(fails + 3, 64)
                if fails >= 35:
                    # Escaping restart: small multiplicative noise around best.
                    if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 5.0:
                        break
                    noise = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, size=best.size))
                    a = _normalize(best * noise)
                    cur_n = int(a.size)
                    L = _next_pow2(3 * cur_n - 1)
                    _, m0 = _fft_score(a)
                    tau = max(1e-12, 1e-3 * max(1e-30, m0))
                    alpha = 0.7
                    fails = 0
            else:
                alpha = min(3.0, alpha * 1.22)
                if acc % 20 == 0:
                    consider(a, force=False)

            if (it & 31) == 0:
                tau *= 0.92
                if tau < 1e-12:
                    tau = 1e-12

        consider(a, force=True)

    except Exception:
        pass

    if best is None or best_score != float("inf"):
        final = _normalize(best) if best is not None else _binomial_length(n)
    else:
        final = _binomial_length(n)

    # Final authoritative validation.  If anything goes wrong, return the
    # guaranteed safe near-optimal binomial construction.
    try:
        if final is None or final.size == 0:
            final = _binomial_length(n)
        final = _normalize(final)
        fs = _exact_score(final)
        if not np.isfinite(fs):
            final = _binomial_length(n)
    except Exception:
        final = _binomial_length(n)

    if final.size == 0:
        final = _binomial_length(n)

    return [float(x) for x in final]
```
